
❯ about the formation here are the requirements
  In [Championship Manager: Season 03/04](https://www.google.com/search?q=champ
  ionship+manager:+season+03/04&kgmid=/m/0gz0w9), Sports Interactive
  pre-programmed a diverse array of default formations categorized into
  standard, defensive, and offensive structures. If you are building an exact
  engine clone, you will need to map your default AI choices and user presets
  to this precise framework. [1, 2]
  Below is the comprehensive list of the core default tactics used by the AI
  and available via the "Quick Load" menu: [1]
  ## 1. Standard Formations (4-Defenders)

  *
  * 4-4-2 Standard: The ultimate baseline tactic for the CM/FM game engine.
  Flat back four, flat four-man midfield, two central strikers.
  * 4-4-2 Diamond: Features one defensive midfielder (DM), two flat central
  midfielders (MC), and one attacking midfielder (AMC).
  * 4-4-2 Attacking: Subtly pushes the two wide midfielders into full winger
  positions (AMR/AML).
  * 4-3-3: A back four supporting three flat central midfielders and three
  direct forwards (ST).
  * 4-5-1: Highly compact engine variant. Utilizes a back four, three central
  midfielders, two wide wingers, and a lone striker.
  * 4-2-3-1: Two holding midfielders (DMs), three attacking midfielders (AMR,
  AMC, AML), and one central striker. [1, 3]
  *

  ## 2. 3-Defender & Wingback Systems

  *
  * 3-5-2 Attacking: Consists of three central defenders (DCs), two wingbacks
  (WBR/WBL), two central midfielders, one distinct attacking midfielder (AMC),
  and two strikers. [4]
  * 3-5-2 Defensive: Lowers the wingbacks into full defensive positions (DR/DL)
  or turns the AMC into a true defensive anchor (DMC).
  * 3-4-3: Three flat defenders, four flat midfielders across the center line,
  and three physical top-line strikers. [5]
  * 5-3-2: A heavy sweeper system utilizing three central defenders flanked by
  traditional fullbacks, anchoring a three-man midfield and two strikers.
  *

  ## 3. Asymmetrical & Non-Traditional Presets

  *
  * 4-1-2-1-2: A variation of the narrow diamond prioritized heavily in the
  CM03/04 tactical engine.
  * 4-2-4: A hyper-aggressive preset featuring a standard back line, two core
  central midfielders, two wide wingers, and two direct target strikers.
  * 5-4-1 (Sweeper): The absolute maximum defensive shape used by low-tier AI
  teams seeking to park the bus.
  * 2-5-3: The maximum hyper-offensive system available in the default files.
  [2, 6, 7]
  *

  ------------------------------
  ## Core Structural Parameters for Your Code Engine
  To build your clone accurately, each of these tactics must read an array of
  global Team Instructions and positional Player Instructions: [8]

  | Engine Setting | Available Values | Game Engine Impact |
  |---|---|---|
  | Mentality | Ultra Defensive, Defensive, Normal, Attacking, Gung Ho |
  Dictates defensive lines, line height, and hazard tracking. |
  | Passing Style | Short, Direct, Long, Mixed | Impacts completion metrics,
  intercept logic, and tempo. |
  | Tackling | Easy, Normal, Hard | Modifies card frequency, injury
  probabilities, and foul rates. |
  | Closing Down | Always, Stand Off, Own Half Only | Governs pressing
  thresholds and player positioning matrices. |
  | Defensive Settings | Offside Trap (Yes/No), Counter Attack (Yes/No), Men
  Behind Ball (Yes/No) | Hardcoded rules overrides for the visual match
  rendering logic. |

  Would you like the exact positional coordinates (X/Y grid maps) for any
  specific formation on the tactical screen, or do you need help mapping the
  With Ball/Without Ball movement logic?

⏺ I haven't changed anything yet. This list goes well beyond the current
  Formation model, and some of it is wrong about CM 03/04, so a few decisions
  come first.

  How formations work today (tactics.ts:3-29
  (packages/shared/src/rules/tactics.ts#L3-L29), CONTEXT.md:245
  (CONTEXT.md#L245)):
  - There are five templates: 4-4-2, 4-3-3, 4-5-1, 3-5-2 and 5-3-2. A manager
    can move any outfield slot, which gives a "custom" shape.
  - There are 10 Positions: GK, DC, DL, DR, DM, MC, ML, MR, AMC, ST. There is no
    AML/AMR, no WBL/WBR and no sweeper.
  - There are three Team Instructions, each with three states: Mentality, Tempo
    and Pressing.
  - The DB has a CHECK constraint that hard-codes the formation and instruction
    values (schema.ts:625-628 (apps/desktop/src/main/db/schema.ts#L625-L628)).

  What the list needs, from least to most work:
  1. New formations that use only existing Positions. These are 4-4-2 Diamond,
     4-1-2-1-2, 4-3-3 with three flat MCs and three STs, 3-4-3, 5-4-1 and 2-5-3.
     Each is a new entry in FORMATIONS/FORMATION_SLOTS plus a migration that
     widens the CHECK constraint. This is small.
  2. Formations that need new Positions. 4-4-2 Attacking, 4-2-3-1 and 4-2-4 need
     AML/AMR. 3-5-2 Attacking needs WBL/WBR. 5-4-1 (Sweeper) needs SW. A new
     Position touches Position Weights, Phase Strength, familiarity, player
     generation, Roles, scouting and the squad/search columns. This is the
     biggest item.
  3. The instruction table. It asks for five-state Mentality, Passing Style,
     Tackling, Closing Down and three on/off toggles. That replaces the current
     three sliders and their Tactical Modifier multipliers, and changes the
     match engine and the save format.

  Accuracy problems in the list. It reads like AI-generated text and the
  citations don't back it up.
  - The 4-3-3 with three flat MCs and three STs is not how CM usually set it up.
    It normally used wide forwards.
  - "2-5-3" as the maximum attacking default is doubtful.
  - "3-5-2 Defensive" gives two different definitions.
  - "Closing Down: Own Half Only" and "Passing Style: Mixed" don't match CM
    03/04's actual settings.

  I'd check it against a real source before copying it into the domain.

  My recommendation: do item 1 now as a normal code change. Items 2 and 3 add
  domain concepts and change the save format and the match engine, so under
  AGENTS.md they go through the planning chain (/wayfinder → spec → tickets).

  Questions for you:
  - Should I do item 1 now?
  - Do you want new Positions (AML/AMR, WBL/WBR, SW) in scope at all?
  - Should the instruction overhaul be part of this, or a separate effort?
  - Should I run /research on the real CM 03/04 formation and instruction set
    first, so the list rests on primary sources?

⏺ I haven't written anything yet. Before I chart the map, two findings change
  its shape:

  1. An effort for the Position side already exists.
     .scratch/player-positional-model/map.md owns the question of which
     Positions exist (SW, WB, wide DM/AM, F vs ST). That includes the tactical
     slot vocabulary in its ticket 05. Its research ticket 01 on the CM 03/04
     editor hasn't started, and five of its tickets wait on it. It has already
     decided that saves are not migrated: old saves are refused.
  2. Roles aren't a CM 03/04 concept. The v1 Roles (Poacher, Anchorman,
     Ball-Playing Defender) come from later Football Manager games. CM 03/04
     used per-player instructions instead. "No MVP compromises" pulls against
     keeping Roles.

  First round of questions:

  ❓ Q1 - How this relates to player-positional-model: Should I (a) chart a new
  sibling effort, formations-and-instructions, that consumes the positional
  effort's slot vocabulary and waits on its ticket 05 for any formation needing
  new slots, or (b) fold both into one map?

  ➡️ (a). The positional effort already has a clean question and a decision
  recorded. This effort owns formation presets, team instructions, player
  instructions, AI tactic choice and the Tactics UI. Formations that use only
  today's Positions can proceed without waiting.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - What decides the formation and instruction lists: Should the lists
  come from the pasted list as written, or from what CM 03/04 actually shipped,
  confirmed by research (the game's default tactic files and the tactics and
  instruction screens)?

  ➡️ What CM 03/04 shipped, confirmed by research. The pasted list becomes the
  starting guess the research checks. The research ticket should share work with
  the positional effort's ticket 01, since both read the same game.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Roles: Should we (a) keep the v1 Roles alongside the CM settings, (b)
  replace them with CM 03/04's per-player instructions (forward runs, run with
  ball, long shots, through balls, crossing, marking, tackling, closing down,
  free role and so on), or (c) keep both?

  ➡️ (b). This overturns the Role entries in CONTEXT.md and the
  role-rating-outside-match-engine Agent Note, and the docs get reconciled in
  the same change as the code.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - Match engine fidelity: The engine today is a three-phase strength
  model with no player positions on a pitch. CM 03/04's tactics screen had
  separate with-ball and without-ball positions (movement arrows) and settings
  like the offside trap, which only mean something in a spatial engine. Should
  we (a) keep the three-phase engine and map every setting to phase and modifier
  effects, or (b) plan a spatial match engine?

  ➡️ (a) for this effort. Every setting still has a real, tested effect, and a
  spatial engine becomes its own effort, listed as out of scope. (b) is a
  rewrite several times larger than everything else here. Say so if "no
  compromises" means (b).

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - Mid-match changes: Today only Team Instructions can change during a
  match. CM let you change formation, move players and change instructions at
  any stoppage. Should we allow full Tactic changes mid-match?

  ➡️ Yes: formation, slots and every instruction can change live. The bench and
  the substitution rules stay as they are.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Saved tactics: CM had named tactic files and a Quick Load menu of
  presets. Should we add a per-manager library of named, saved Tactics next to
  the built-in presets?

  ➡️ Yes.

  ---

  ❓ Q7 - AI clubs' tactics: Should AI clubs choose a formation and instructions
  from their squad and the opponent, and adjust mentality during a match based
  on the score and the minute?

  ➡️ Yes. That's the "AI default choices" part of your requirements.

  ---

  ❓ Q8 - Save compatibility: Should this effort follow the positional effort's
  decision to refuse old saves rather than migrate them?

  ➡️ Yes, for consistency.

❯ Understood. These answers establish a separate sibling effort named
  formations-and-instructions, with the following binding decisions:

  Effort boundary

  The new effort owns:

  Built-in formation presets
  Team instructions
  Per-player instructions
  Live tactical changes
  Named saved tactics
  AI tactic selection and adjustment
  Tactics UI
  Persistence and validation of complete tactics
  Reconciliation of documentation that currently describes Roles

  It consumes the slot vocabulary defined by:

  .scratch/player-positional-model/map.md


  It does not absorb or duplicate the positional effort.

  Key decisions

  Sibling effort, not merged

  Formations using existing Positions can proceed immediately.
  Any formation requiring a new tactical slot depends on ticket 05 of
  player-positional-model.

  CM 03/04 is the source of truth

  The pasted formation and instruction lists are research hypotheses only.
  Research must inspect the actual game, including default tactic files and the
  relevant tactics and instruction screens.
  Research should be coordinated with ticket 01 of player-positional-model to
  avoid examining the same artifacts twice.

  Roles are removed

  Poacher, Anchorman, Ball-Playing Defender, and similar later-era Football
  Manager Roles are not retained.
  They are replaced by CM 03/04-style per-player instructions.
  CONTEXT.md, Agent Notes, domain models, UI, ratings logic, fixtures, and
  tests must be reconciled in the same implementation sequence.
  There should be no compatibility adapter that silently converts Roles into
  instruction bundles unless research establishes that such presets are useful
  as UI-only conveniences. They must not remain domain concepts.

  The current match engine remains non-spatial

  Formation and instruction settings map to explicit, deterministic, tested
  effects in the current three-phase engine.
  A spatial match engine is a separate future effort.
  Unsupported spatial semantics must not be faked. For example, movement arrows
  may require a documented phase-based approximation rather than pretending
  that coordinates are simulated.

  Complete tactics can change during matches

  Formation
  Slot assignments
  Team instructions
  Every player instruction

  These changes become effective at the next valid stoppage or tactical
  application boundary. Existing bench and substitution rules remain unchanged.

  Managers receive a named tactic library

  Built-in presets remain available.
  Managers can create, name, update, duplicate, delete, and quick-load custom
  tactics.
  Saved tactics must contain enough information to reconstruct the complete
  tactical state, not just the formation name.

  AI controls complete tactics

  AI clubs choose tactics from squad capabilities and opponent characteristics.
  AI may adjust mentality and other instructions according to score, minute,
  match state, and available players.
  AI choices must use the same public domain operations and validation rules as
  human-manager changes.

  Old saves are refused

  No migration from Role-based or pre-tactic saves.
  Save compatibility behavior must match player-positional-model.
  The rejection should identify the unsupported save version clearly rather
  than failing during deserialization.
  Recommended map structure
  01-research-cm0304-formations-and-instructions
  02-define-tactic-domain-model
  03-remove-role-domain-model
  04-implement-built-in-formation-presets
  05-implement-team-instructions
  06-implement-player-instructions
  07-map-tactics-to-match-engine-effects
  08-implement-live-tactical-changes
  09-implement-saved-tactic-library
  10-implement-ai-tactic-selection
  11-implement-ai-mid-match-adjustments
  12-build-tactics-ui
  13-integrate-save-version-rejection
  14-reconcile-documentation-and-agent-notes
  15-system-validation-and-regression

  Critical dependency shape
  player-positional-model/01 ─┬─ shared CM 03/04 research
                              └─ formations-and-instructions/01

  formations-and-instructions/01
      ├─ 02 domain model
      ├─ 04 formation presets
      ├─ 05 team instructions
      └─ 06 player instructions

  player-positional-model/05
      └─ only blocks presets that require newly introduced slots

  02 + 03 + 04 + 05 + 06
      └─ 07 match-engine effects
          ├─ 08 live changes
          ├─ 10 AI initial choice
          └─ 11 AI match adjustments

  02 + 04 + 05 + 06
      ├─ 09 saved tactic library
      └─ 12 tactics UI

  all implementation tickets
      ├─ 13 save rejection
      ├─ 14 documentation reconciliation
      └─ 15 system validation


  The main architectural guardrail is that formation presets, tactical slots,
  player assignments, and instructions must be distinct concepts. A formation
  describes available slots; a tactic combines a formation, assigned players,
  team instructions, and per-player instructions. This avoids turning preset
  names such as 4-4-2 into mutable tactical state.

  like "implement live tactical changes" are implementation slices, which
  to-tickets cuts from the spec. So I'll chart the decision behind each of your
  items, and record your 04-15 list in the map as the slice plan the spec has to
  produce. I'm reading the positional research ticket first so the two research
  passes don't overlap.

      10 +

         +. Does it also carry default                                   
      12 +  instructions (CM's `.tac` files may), or only slots?

      14 +  [player-positional-model ticket 05](../../player-positional-m

         +l cells (ticket 02, item 5)?

         +uctions + per-player instructions +

         +struction follow the player when he

› Message from a83f1fbc352f600ed
  I wrote the findings to /Users/joao/dev/audit/docs/research/player-positional-
  model-cm0304-positional-fields.md and added the "Findings:" link to the
  ticket, leaving Status: claimed. verify-md-links passes. I ran no git
  commands. I did the research myself instead of starting a background agent.

  **The eight items:**

  1. **Storage** (verified for CM 03/04): each line and each side is its own
  rating. There is no value per pitch cell and no stored label or preferred
  position. The ratings are signed bytes, and the editor allows 0–20. Ratings
  can change through "Retrain Position/Side" training.
  2. **Lines** (verified): Goalkeeper, Sweeper, Defender, Defensive Midfielder,
  Midfielder, Attacking Midfielder, Attacker, Wing Back, Free Role. The editor
  has one Attacker field. The in-game `F`/`S` is worked out from it: `F` when
  Left, Right, Free Role or AM is also ≥ 15, otherwise `S`.
  3. **Sides** (verified): Right, Left, Centre, stored in that order and
  labelled R/L/C. Sweeper takes sides (`SW/D C`). Goalkeepers only ever show as
  bare `GK`. Whether the label hides GK sides on purpose is unverified; the CM
  01/02 tool does hide them.
  4. **Free Role** (verified): three separate things. A hidden 1–20 player
  rating stored with the positions, a per-player tactical instruction, and a
  coaching preference field on non-playing staff. As a rating it feeds the
  `F`/`S` label rule. In CM 01/02 it also counts toward the AMC slot penalty
  (inherited, not checked for CM 03/04).
  5. **Slot suitability**: unverified. No source gives how a line and a side
  combine for one slot. In CM 01/02 the value is worked out per slot and has
  special cases, for example wide DM slots read Wing Back and M slots use max(M,
  AM−5) (inherited). Community tools test line ≥ 15 AND side ≥ 15. `min` stays
  a candidate only.
  6. **Labels** (verified as a tool's reconstruction, consistent with 224
  in-game CM 03/04 labels):
     - A line or side shows at ≥ 15, and GK on its own overrides everything.
     - Lines run SW/D/DM/M/AM/F-or-S, joined by `/`. M hides when DM or AM
  qualifies, and AM hides when DM qualifies.
     - Sides follow a space, run together in R-L-C order.
     - Wing Back never appears. The ticket's `D/WB L` example was not found
  anywhere; `AM/F RC` and `D/DM RC` are real.
  7. **Consequence**: undocumented for CM 03/04. In CM 01/02 (inherited),
  tactical attributes such as positioning, marking, anticipation, off the ball
  and creativity are cut when the slot or arrowed-to slot is rated below 20, and
  lower Versatility means a bigger cut. The six FM suitability tiers are later
  Football Manager.
  8. **Differences**: CM4 and CM 03/04 use the same data format. CM 01/02 stores
  the same fields in a different order (Free Role last). In the CM 01/02 tool a
  Wing Back rating counts toward the `AM` label; the CM 03/04 tool ignores Wing
  Back. Whether that is a real game change is unverified.

  **Sources:**
  - Nygreen's CM 4 Pregame Editor and CM Scout Delphi source
  (sourceforge.net/projects/cmeditors), the nearest thing to primary evidence.
  - Three CM 03/04 GameFAQs guides (faqs 28326, 28438, 30572).
  - CM 01/02 tools: CM0102Patcher, CMScoutIntrinsicCommunity, CMScouterCore.
  - MadScientist's "FAQ: Positional Penalty" thread on champman0102.co.uk
  (t=2321), plus threads t=3004, 2773, 10369 and 6259.

  The findings file has a "Leads for sibling tickets" section: the CM 03/04
  formation list stored in the data, the shipped preset names, individual
  instructions, and how arrows work.

⏺ The research on how CM 03/04 stores player positions is finished, and I've
  marked that ticket resolved. The findings are in
  docs/research/player-positional-model-cm0304-positional-fields.md, and a
  one-line summary is on the positional map
  (.scratch/player-positional-model/map.md). It's fact-finding only, so it has
  no Agent Note. The link check passes.

  Confirmed for CM 03/04:
  - A player has nine separate position ratings from 0 to 20: GK, SW, D, DM, M,
    AM, Attacker, Wing Back and Free Role. He also has three side ratings:
    right, left and centre. Nothing is stored per pitch cell.
  - A position label shows a line or side rated 15 or above. F or S is worked
    out from the Attacker rating.
  - Wing Back never appears in a label, so the ticket's D/WB L example isn't
    real.
  - Free Role is two things at once: a hidden player rating and a per-player
    instruction.

  Not confirmed:
  - How a line rating and a side rating combine into a player's fit for one
    slot. CM 01/02 had special cases per slot, such as wide DM slots reading the
    Wing Back rating.
  - What the match engine does to a player out of position. It's only documented
    for CM 01/02, where some tactical attributes are reduced, more so for
    players with low Versatility.

  What this means for the formations effort:
  - Free Role belongs to both efforts: the positional effort's Free Role ticket
    (04) and my player-instructions ticket (07).
  - The penalty for playing out of position feeds my engine-mapping ticket (08).

  The formation-presets and instructions research (tickets 02 and 03) is still
  running.

› Message from a5ed1cc9642d5e4e9
  Ticket 02 is answered from the game's own files: the retail CD image (v4.1.0)
  and patches 4.1.4 and 4.1.5, all from an Internet Archive item. I extracted
  the shipped `.tac` files, the tactics screen XML and the executable, and
  decoded them. The findings are in `/Users/joao/dev/audit/docs/research/formati
  ons-and-instructions-cm0304-formations-and-tactic-files.md`. I added the
  Findings line to the ticket and left `Status: claimed`. The link check passes.
  I did no git operations and wrote no other files in the repo.

  **Presets (verified CM 03/04).**
  - **Retail 4.1.0 ships 27**, and the exe checks for exactly 27. 17 of them are
  named `_CM4` internally, and the exe refuses to overwrite "CM4 pre-defined
  tactics", so the set is inherited from CM4.
  - **Patch 4.1.3 onward ships 29**, adding 4-4-1-1 and 4-2-3-1-dk, and the exe
  checks for 29. The changelog says: "Default and computer team tactics refined,
  also added … 4411 and 4231 (Danish version)".
  - **The 29:** 4-4-2, 4-4-2 Attacking/Defensive/Diamond, 4-4-1-1, 4-5-1, 4-3-3,
  4-3-2-1, 4-2-4, 4-2-3-1, 4-2-3-1-dk, 4-1-3-2, 4-1-3-1-1, 4-1-2-1-2, 3-5-2,
  3-4-3, 3-4-2-1, 3-4-1-2, 3-3-4, 3-2-5, 5-4-1, 5-3-2, 5-3-2
  Attacking/Defensive/Sweeper, 5-2-3, 2-5-3, 2-4-4, 2-3-5.
  - **Slot layouts:** the findings file has full tables for both versions,
  giving each player's base cell and run target.
  - **Retail bug:** 4-4-2, 4-4-2 Defensive and 4-4-2 Diamond are byte-identical
  apart from the name. 4.1.3 turned the Diamond into DM C / M R / M L / AM C.

  **Checked against the requirements list:**
  - 3-5-2 Attacking and 3-5-2 Defensive don't exist; the game ships 5-3-2
  Attacking and 5-3-2 Defensive.
  - There is no 5-4-1 Sweeper; the only sweeper preset is 5-3-2 Sweeper.
  - 2-5-3 did ship.
  - 4-3-3 really is three central midfielders plus three strikers.

  **Slot grid (verified).** One goalkeeper cell plus six outfield rows (SW, D,
  DM, M, AM, forward) by five columns (L, LC, C, RC, R), 31 cells at most. There
  is no wing-back row. Which side is "R" is unverified.

  **What a `.tac` file holds (verified layout).**
  - Header, name and author.
  - A 9-byte team block.
  - 11 player blocks, each with 24 pitch coordinate pairs, a base cell, a run
  target cell, a 6-byte instruction block and an 8-byte block (probably
  set-piece instructions).
  - A 12-byte trailer.

  There are no player identities, captain or takers in the file. The meaning of
  the individual instruction bits is unverified.

  **AI (partly verified).** The presets double as the computer teams' tactics.
  Staff have a "Preferred Formation" attribute whose 15 values in 4.1.0 are all
  preset names. How an AI manager actually picks a preset is unverified; I found
  no documentation and didn't trace the code.

  **With-ball / without-ball (verified).** Each player has a base cell plus one
  **run** (the XML says "run colour"; players call it the "arrow", set with a
  right-click drag). The game never says "with ball" or "without ball". That
  naming probably comes from later FM (unverified).

  **Sources:**
  - Internet Archive `championship-manager-season-0304`: `CM0304.iso`,
  `cm0304patch414.exe`, `cm0304patch415.exe`
  - The SI changelog inside the patches
  - GameFAQs guides by thy451 and cairo140, via Wayback Machine snapshots

  **Leads for sibling tickets:**
  - **Ticket 03:** `tactical_templates.xml` fully lists the player-instruction
  fields and options, and `tactics.xml` lists the set-piece and priority panels.
  - **Player-positional-model ticket 01:** the exe has "Wing Back", "Full Back"
  and "Winger" position strings, which likely belong to the player database
  rather than the tactics grid.

⏺ The three research tickets and the Role-consumer inventory (ticket 13) are
  resolved, and each is recorded on its map. I've claimed ticket 04, the Tactic
  domain model, which needs your answers. The research overturned several items
  in your pasted list:

  - 29 shipped presets, not 14. "3-5-2 Attacking/Defensive" and "5-4-1 Sweeper"
    don't exist. The game has 5-3-2 Attacking, Defensive and Sweeper. 2-5-3 is
    real, and so is the 4-3-3 with three central midfielders and three strikers.
  - The tactics screen is a grid. It has a GK cell plus six rows (SW, D, DM, M,
    AM, F) across five columns (L, LC, C, RC, R). There is no wing-back row:
    wing-backs are D or DM cells on the flanks. Each player has a base cell and
    one run target, which is the arrow.
  - No sliders anywhere. There are nine team instructions: Passing, Focus
    Passing, Tackling, Closing Down, Mentality (five values), Offside Trap,
    Zonal Marking, Counter Attack and Men Behind The Ball.
    - Players override five of those individually. They also have crossing
      settings, goalkeeper distribution, and seven normal/often flags such as
      Forward Runs, Free Role and Hold Up Ball.
    - Tempo, width, a playmaker and a target man are not in CM 03/04.
  - A tactic file holds cells, runs and per-slot instructions, but no players,
    and no captain or set-piece takers.

  Round 1 for ticket 04:

  ❓ Q1 - What a slot is: Today a slot is one of 10 Positions (DC, MC, ...), so
  two centre-backs are both "DC". CM's slot is a grid cell (row × column), which
  tells D LC from D RC. Should a Tactic's slot be a grid cell?

  ➡️ Yes: GK plus the 6×5 outfield grid. The row and column names belong to the
  positional effort's slot-vocabulary ticket (05), so I'd pass this answer there
  as an input rather than decide the names here.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Runs: Should each slot carry an optional run target cell (CM's arrow)?

  ➡️ Yes, store it; it's part of a CM tactic. Ticket 08 decides its honest
  effect on the engine, for example part of the player's contribution counting
  toward the target row's phase. No pretend movement.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Do instructions follow the slot or the player? CM stores player
  instructions per slot, because tactic files contain no players.

  ➡️ Per slot. A player moved to another slot takes on that slot's instructions.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - Presets and saved tactics are one concept: A CM preset and a
  user-saved tactic have the same format: cells, runs, per-slot instructions and
  team instructions, with no players. Should built-in presets and the manager's
  saved tactics be one domain type, differing only in being read-only and in
  who owns them?

  ➡️ Yes. Call it a Tactic Template. A built-in template is read-only and a
  saved one belongs to the manager. The club's live Tactic is a template's
  contents plus player assignments and the bench. This also meets your
  guardrail: "4-4-2" names a template and never becomes mutable state.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - What a Tactic is called: Preset names don't follow row counts. "4-4-2
  Attacking" has two M and two AM, so counting rows would call it 4-2-2-2.
  Should a Tactic show the name of the template it came from, with a derived
  "modified" marker once it differs (replacing today's "4-4-2 (custom)")? And
  should the row-count label be derived and shown separately?

  ➡️ Yes to both. The Tactic stores the name of its source template; "modified"
  and the row-count label are always derived, never stored.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - The save command: Should ChangeTactics carry the complete Tactic
  (cells, runs, per-slot instructions, team instructions, assignments, bench) as
  one full replacement, still guarded by Expected Revision and Request Id?

  ➡️ Yes: one command, full replacement. There's no command per field, so a
  partial update can never leave a half-applied Tactic.

  ---

  ❓ Q7 - Glossary terms: I propose these CONTEXT.md terms:
  - Tactic: the club's live value.
  - Tactic Template: built-in or saved.
  - Formation: the arrangement of 11 slots with their runs.
  - Slot, Run, Team Instruction and Player Instruction.

  "Preset" would be avoided as a domain term and used only as a UI label. Role,
  Role Weights and Role Rating would be removed.

  ➡️ Accept as listed. CONTEXT.md is updated when the code ships, to match your
  rule that docs change with the code.

❯ approve all

⏺ Round 2 covers three tickets that are now ready, each claimed.

  Ticket 05: the built-in preset set

  ❓ Q1 - Which set ships: Retail CM 03/04 shipped 27 presets. Patch 4.1.3
  reworked 24 of them, fixed a retail bug where 4-4-2, 4-4-2 Defensive and 4-4-2
  Diamond were identical, and added 4-4-1-1 and 4-2-3-1-dk. Should we ship the
  patched set of 29, with each preset's cells and runs exactly as in the files?

  ➡️ Yes, the patched 29. It's the final state SI shipped, and the community
  guides describe it. Display names are the file names as shipped. Where the
  files leave something unconfirmed (the research couldn't pin down which column
  is left and which is right), we take the reading that matches CM's usual
  order.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Nothing ships before the grid: Your earlier boundary said "formations
  using existing Positions can proceed immediately." Ticket 04 made slots grid
  cells, so every preset now depends on the grid. The runs alone use wide DM, AM
  and F cells everywhere. That means every preset waits on the positional
  effort's slot-vocabulary ticket (05). Accept?

  ➡️ Accept. Also, the positional effort's representation ticket (03) is now
  unblocked, so the positional map can move in parallel with this one.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Starting templates: CM staff had a "Preferred Formation" that named a
  preset. Should manager_profile.preferred_formation become a reference to one
  of the 29 built-in templates? The manager's first Tactic would load it, and an
  AI club would start from its manager's preferred template until ticket 11
  decides how the AI chooses.

  ➡️ Yes. The current five-value limit on that field widens to the 29.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - Instruction values in built-in templates: The files' instruction bytes
  couldn't be decoded, so the exact instruction values SI shipped are unknown.
  Should built-in templates ship exact cells and runs, with instructions taken
  from the per-slot defaults ticket 07 decides? The alternative is guessing
  values from undecoded bytes.

  ➡️ Take the ticket 07 defaults. Nothing we can't confirm gets presented as
  SI's data.

  Ticket 06: the team instruction set

  ❓ Q5 - The set: Should the three sliders (Mentality, Tempo, Pressing) be
  replaced by CM's nine team instructions, with the game's unticked state as
  each default?
  - Passing: mixed, short, direct, long.
  - Focus Passing: mixed, both flanks, left flank, right flank, through the
    middle.
  - Tackling: normal, easy, hard.
  - Closing Down: default, own half only, always.
  - Mentality: normal, ultra defensive, defensive, attacking, gung ho.
  - Four on/off switches: Offside Trap, Zonal Marking, Counter Attack, Men
    Behind The Ball.

  ➡️ Yes, exactly those. Tempo and Pressing go; they aren't in CM 03/04. Closing
  Down's "default" is stored as its own value, because the game never said what
  it means; ticket 08 gives it an effect.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Tactical Style presets: Career creation picks a style (gegenpress,
  tiki-taka, catenaccio and others) that seeds the three sliders. These are
  modern names, and two of the three axes they seed stop existing. Should we (a)
  remove the style and let the preferred template from Q3 be the manager's
  tactical identity, or (b) remap each style onto CM team instructions?

  ➡️ (a). CM 03/04 had nothing like a style, and the preferred formation already
  covers the identity. This partly supersedes the manager-style-and-appearance
  note (2026-09-27) and its career-creation picker. The appearance half stays.

  Ticket 10: the saved tactic library

  ❓ Q7 - Where the library lives: CM's .tac files sat on disk and were shared
  by every save. Should the library be (a) in the save, owned by the manager and
  following them between clubs, or (b) an app-level folder shared across saves,
  like CM?

  ➡️ (a). The save is this app's unit of state. Export and import across saves
  can come later.

  ---

  ❓ Q8 - What quick load does to players: A template holds no players. When one
  with a different shape is loaded, should the player in slot n stay in slot n
  (which moves to its new cell, as CM's slot order implies), or should Best XI
  re-seat everyone?

  ➡️ Keep players by slot number, with the bench untouched. The manager then
  adjusts. Auto-reseating would quietly override choices the manager made.

  ---

  ❓ Q9 - Library operations: Create from the current Tactic, rename, overwrite
  from the current Tactic, duplicate, delete, and quick load. Built-in templates
  are read-only and can only be duplicated. Names are unique per manager,
  ignoring case, and can't reuse a built-in name. There is no cap on entries.
  Every operation carries a Request Id so a retry is harmless, and overwrite,
  rename and delete also carry an Expected Revision per template.

  ➡️ Accept as listed.

⏺ Round 3 covers ticket 07, player instructions and what replaces Role Rating.

  ❓ Q1 - The set: Should we adopt exactly CM 03/04's per-player screen?
  - Five settings a player can take from the team or override:
    - Passing: mixed, short, direct, long.
    - Closing Down: stand off, own half only, always.
    - Tackling: easy, normal, hard.
    - Marking: zonal, man, specific.
    - Mentality: all five values.

    Each of these stores an explicit team value, which is CM's "(same as team)",
    unticked state.
  - Three settings with no team counterpart: Distribution (long kick, ask
    defenders to collect), Cross From (deep, touchline), Cross Aim (near post,
    centre, far post, man). Each has a default value.
  - Seven normal/often switches: Cross Ball, Long Shots, Forward Runs, Run With
    Ball, Try Through Balls, Free Role, Hold Up Ball.

  ➡️ Yes, exactly that. There is no team-level crossing instruction, because the
  game showed none.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Distribution: CM only gave Distribution to goalkeepers. Should it be
  settable only on the GK slot, with validation rejecting any other value
  elsewhere?

  ➡️ Yes. The GK is always slot 0, so the rule is simple.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - "Specific" marking: This setting names an opposing player. A template
  holds no players, and the opponent changes every match. Should it be a
  match-time setting? It would be set before kick-off or live, stored with that
  match's tactic application, and dropped at full time. Templates and the stored
  Tactic would allow only team, zonal and man.

  ➡️ Yes. Otherwise the stored Tactic points at players from last week's
  opponent.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - Default instructions per slot: CM shipped seven instruction templates:
  Goalkeeper, Central Defender, Full Back, Defensive Midfielder, Attacking
  Midfielder, Winger and Striker. They sit behind the player screen's "Set To
  Preset" button. Their values are in the shipped tactical_templates.xml and can
  be decoded from its documented header, but the research deliberately didn't
  copy the numbers. My proposal:
  - Add an AFK task ticket that writes those seven templates' values down,
    verified against the file.
  - Built-in templates give each slot the instruction template that matches its
    cell:
    - GK → Goalkeeper.
    - SW and central D → Central Defender.
    - D L/R → Full Back.
    - DM → Defensive Midfielder.
    - AM C → Attacking Midfielder.
    - Wide M and AM → Winger.
    - F → Striker.
    - Central M → all defaults, because CM had no central-midfielder template.
  - The seven also appear as a "Set To Preset" button on the player panel: a UI
    shortcut that fills in values. The Roles note allows this, because CM itself
    had it.

  ➡️ Accept as listed.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - What replaces Role Rating's ±0.05 nudge: Should we (a) drop the fit
  measure entirely, with ticket 08 making each instruction's effect depend on
  the executing player's attributes (Long Shots "often" pays off in proportion
  to the player's shooting ability), or (b) invent an "instruction fit" rating?

  ➡️ (a). CM showed no fit rating. Tying effects to the player's attributes is
  how a bad instruction for the wrong player hurts, without a second rating
  system. The Tactics screen shows positional suitability from the positional
  effort where Role Rating used to be.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Free Role: In CM it's both a per-player switch and a hidden player
  rating. That rating belongs to the positional effort's Free Role ticket (04).
  Should the switch be the instruction here, with ticket 08 scaling its effect
  by that rating if the positional effort keeps it, and by attributes if not?

  ➡️ Yes. That makes ticket 08 also wait on the positional Free Role ticket.

❯ approve all

  instruction-template values.

› Message from a6a2896059f87dde4
  I added an `## Answer` section to `/Users/joao/dev/audit/.scratch/formations-a
  nd-instructions/issues/14-transcribe-cm-instruction-templates.md`. `Status:
  claimed` is unchanged, the link check passes (1494 files), I did no git
  operations, and all game files stayed under /tmp/cm0304.

  **Where the file came from.** I pulled a fresh copy from the retail ISO by
  carving cabinet folder 98 out of Data1.cab and extracting it with tar. It is
  5,354 bytes, SHA-1 `1d94ac21…`. Patch 4.1.4 stores its copy uncompressed as
  `\data\tactics\tactical_templates.xml`, and that copy is **byte-identical**
  (same SHA-1).

  **How the values decode.** The file's header says 0 or an omitted field means
  "team default, or false".
  - On the five override rows (Passing, Closing Down, Tackling, Marking,
  Mentality), 0 means `team`.
  - On Distribution, Cross From and Cross Aim, 0 means `default`.
  - For the seven flags, false means `normal` and true means `often`.
  - No field is ever omitted: every record declares all 15.

  | Instruction | GK | CD | FB | DM | AM | Wing | ST |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Passing | 3 direct | 3 direct | 3 direct | 1 mixed | 2 short | 1 mixed | 3
  direct |
  | Closing Down | 1 stand off | 1 stand off | 1 stand off | 3 always | 0 team |
  1 stand off | 0 team |
  | Tackling | 2 normal | 2 normal | 2 normal | 2 normal | 2 normal | 2 normal |
  2 normal |
  | Marking | 1 zonal | 2 man | 1 zonal | 2 man | 0 team | 1 zonal | 0 team |
  | Mentality | 3 normal | 2 defensive | 3 normal | 2 defensive | 4 attacking |
  4 attacking | 4 attacking |
  | Distribution | 1 long kick | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | Cross From | 0 | 0 | 1 deep | 0 | 0 | 2 touchline | 0 |
  | Cross Aim | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 near post | 0 |
  | Often | none | none | Cross Ball, Forward Runs, Run With Ball | Long Shots,
  Forward Runs | Long Shots, Run With Ball, Try Through Balls, Free Role | Cross
  Ball, Forward Runs, Run With Ball | Run With Ball, Hold Up Ball |

  **Mapping problems:**
  1. **Seeding every built-in slot from these templates would cut the slots off
  from the Team Instructions.** All seven templates set Passing, Tackling and
  Mentality explicitly. If built-in slots start from them, those three Team
  Instructions would only reach central-M slots, which start at the defaults. In
  CM a template applied only when the user pressed "Set To Preset". This needs
  a decision against ticket 07.
  2. **Tackling is stored as 2 (normal) in every template, never 0.** That makes
  "normal" an explicit override rather than `team`.
  3. **The file calls 0 "team default" on Distribution, Cross From and Cross Aim
  as well.** The note calls that state `default`, and the earlier research
  suspects a hidden team crossing setting exists. `default` is only the right
  name if it means "whatever the engine does with no instruction".
  4. **Marking has no code for specific marking.** That matches the note, which
  allows only `team`, zonal and man in templates.
  5. **Template names carry localisation tags** (`[COMMENT: tactical_templates;
  Position]`). The display name is the text before the bracket.

⏺ Agent "Transcribe CM instruction templates" finished · 2m 3s

⏺ Ticket 14 is resolved: all seven templates are decoded from the shipped file,
  and patch 4.1.4's copy is identical. The values show that one ticket 07 answer
  you approved (Q4) doesn't work as written, so I'm reopening it before
  anything builds on it.

  The problem: every CM template sets Passing, Tackling and Mentality
  explicitly. For example, the Striker template sets Mentality to "attacking"
  and Tackling to "normal". If built-in presets start each slot from its
  template, as approved, the Team Instructions stop reaching those slots.
  Setting the team's Mentality to "defensive" would change only the central
  midfielders, the one slot with no CM template. In CM itself a template applied
  only when you pressed "Set To Preset" on a player.

  ❓ Q1 - Default instructions on built-in slots: Pick one:
  - (a) Every slot starts at team, default and normal. The seven templates
    appear only on the "Set To Preset" button. This is closest to how CM
    behaved.
  - (b) Keep the approved rule: seed every slot from its template. The team's
    Passing, Tackling and Mentality then mostly do nothing on built-in presets.
  - (c) Seed only what has no team counterpart: Distribution, Cross From, Cross
    Aim and the seven switches. The five override settings stay on team. A
    striker starts with Hold Up Ball and Run With Ball on "often", and still
    follows the team's Mentality.

  ➡️ (c). Players keep behaving differently by position, which you'd lose with
  (a), and Team Instructions still reach every slot, which you'd lose with (b).
  The full template stays available through "Set To Preset".

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - What default means: The file calls the unset value "team default" on
  Distribution, Cross From and Cross Aim too, though the team screen has no
  crossing or distribution row. Should default mean "the engine's own behaviour
  with no instruction", with ticket 08 deciding that behaviour?

  ➡️ Yes. There's no team value it could inherit.

  Once you answer, I'll amend ticket 07 and its Agent Note. After that I'll
  start the positional grilling if you want it, as proposed in my last message.
  Nothing is committed yet.

⏺ I've amended ticket 07 and its Agent Note with your two answers. Built-in
  slots now take only the non-override instructions from CM's templates, and
  default means the engine's own behaviour.

  Positional round 1 covers that effort's ticket 03, what a player stores about
  positions, and ticket 04, Free Role. I've claimed both. The research found:
  - CM 03/04 stores independent line ratings (GK, SW, D, DM, M, AM, Attacker,
    Wing Back) and side ratings (R, L, C), each 0–20.
  - Free Role is a hidden 0–20 rating.
  - Wing Back never appears in a label. In CM 01/02 it decides who fits the wide
    DM cells.

  Ticket 03: the representation

  ❓ Q1 - Line × side, as CM stored it: Should a player carry eight independent
  line ratings (GK, SW, D, DM, M, AM, F, WB) and three independent side ratings
  (R, L, C), replacing today's Position and Familiarity Tier rows? The other
  option is one rating per pitch cell, which CM never stored.

  ➡️ Line × side. It's what CM stored, and it keeps the compact label a clean
  grid.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Scale and status: Should these ratings run 1–20, the same range as
  Attributes? They would be stored like Attributes and changed only by Player
  Development or retraining, which is still undecided. But they wouldn't be
  Attributes in the glossary's sense, since they aren't skills. The glossary
  would call them Line Rating and Side Rating.

  ➡️ Yes. CM's editor allowed 0, but 1–20 matches every other rating in the
  game, and 1 already means "can't play there".

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Storage: Should player_positions (one row per player and Position) be
  replaced by one row per player, with eleven rating columns each limited to
  1–20?

  ➡️ Yes. Every player always has all eleven, so a row per rating would only add
  joins. Saves are refused, not migrated, as already decided.

  Ticket 04: Free Role

  ❓ Q4 - The Free Role rating: Should Free Role be stored as a twelfth 1–20
  rating beside the lines, hidden from every screen as CM hid it, and read only
  by the rules?
  - the label's F-or-S rule;
  - AM C suitability (ticket 07 decides whether to adopt CM 01/02's rule);
  - the size of the Free Role instruction's effect (formations ticket 08).

  ➡️ Yes, hidden. The coach's "Free Roles" preference on staff is out of scope
  for this effort.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - Wing Back: Should the WB rating be stored even though no label ever
  shows it? It would feed the wide DM and wide D cells' suitability in ticket
  07.

  ➡️ Yes. Without it, the 5-3-2 family's wing-backs, who stand in DM R/L, have
  no rating to fit against.

  Still open: do you want the planning files committed? I'd commit only this
  session's paths and leave the other session's uncommitted work alone.

⏺ Committed as ad3d3828: the formations tickets 04–07, 10, 13 and 14, positional
  tickets 03 and 04, and five proposed Agent Notes. The commit contains only
  planning files, so I ran the link check (it passes) but not the full
  check:all. An earlier snapshot of the formations map, tickets and research had
  already gone into the other session's 57b74977. ad3d3828 brings those files
  up to date without rewriting history.

  I've claimed positional tickets 05, 07 and 08. This is round 2 of the
  positional grilling.

  Positional ticket 05: slot vocabulary

  ❓ Q1 - How a slot is named: Should a slot be its own type, a pair of (row,
  column), and not a Position?
  - Rows reuse the player line codes, except WB, which is never a row: GK, SW,
    D, DM, M, AM, F.
  - Columns are L, LC, C, RC, R.
  - Slots display as D RC, AM L and so on, and sort in pitch order.

  ➡️ Yes. Rows share the codes of the lines they're rated against, so one
  vocabulary covers both.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Position Weights: The attribute weights behind Position Rating are
  keyed by today's 10 Positions. Should they be keyed by (row, wide or central)
  instead? L and R count as wide; LC, C and RC count as central. That gives 12
  tables:
  - 8 carry over from today: D wide is today's DL/DR, D central is DC, DM
    central is DM, M wide is ML/MR, M central is MC, AM central is AMC, F
    central is ST, and GK stays GK.
  - 4 are new and need authored weights: SW, DM wide (the wing-back), AM wide
    and F wide.

  ➡️ Yes. Weights are design values, written in the spec rather than taken from
  research.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Which phase a slot feeds: Should phase follow the row? GK, SW and D
  feed defence; DM and M feed midfield; AM and F feed attack. A wing-back in DM
  L/R would then feed midfield.

  ➡️ Yes, by row. Whether a run moves part of a player's contribution to its
  target row's phase is for formations ticket 08.

  Positional ticket 07: suitability and Familiarity Tier

  ❓ Q4 - The suitability rule: CM 03/04's rule is unknown, so this becomes the
  game's own rule, informed by CM 01/02:

  suitability(cell) = min(line for the row, side for the column)

  - LC and RC read the C side.
  - D L/R and DM L/R read max(line, WB).
  - M reads max(M, AM − 5), CM 01/02's rule.
  - GK reads only the GK line.

  ➡️ Accept, labelled in the spec as this game's rule, not CM's.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - Familiarity Tier: Should it stay, derived from suitability: natural at
  18–20, competent at 15–17 (which also matches the label threshold),
  unfamiliar at 14 or below? Overall Rating would stay "the best Position Rating
  among Natural cells".

  ➡️ Yes. Overall Rating, Transfer Value and the Tactics overview keep working
  unchanged, and the numbers are marked as this game's thresholds.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Penalty for playing out of position: The positional map explicitly
  ruled a match-engine penalty out of its own scope. CM did penalise it: CM
  01/02 cut tactical attributes when a player's rating for the slot was below
  20, scaled by Versatility. Should formations ticket 08 now own that penalty,
  reading suitability, with the positional map's out-of-scope line redirected
  there?

  ➡️ Yes. A CM clone where playing a striker at centre-back costs nothing isn't
  production-level. Formations 08 is already where every engine effect is
  decided.

  Positional ticket 08: labels

  ❓ Q7 - The label rule: Should we adopt CM Scout's reconstruction as it
  stands? It matches all 224 CM 03/04 labels the guides transcribed:
  - A line or side shows at 15 or above.
  - A GK rating of 15 or above renders as plain GK.
  - Lines appear in the order SW/D/DM/M/AM/F-or-S.
  - M shows only if both DM and AM are below 15.
  - AM shows only if DM is below 15 and (F is below 15 or M is 15 or above).
  - F versus S: F when Left, Right, Free Role or AM is also 15 or above,
    otherwise S.
  - WB never shows.
  - Sides follow a space, in R-L-C order with no separator: D RC, AM RLC.

  ➡️ Adopt it exactly. Worked examples from the research become the rule's
  tests.

       1 +# Agent Note: Slots are row-column cells, weighted by row and w
         +idth                                                           
       2 +                                                               
       3 +Status: proposed                                               
       4 +                                                               
       5 +## Problem                                                     
       6 +                                                               
       7 +Tactic slots become cells on CM 03/04's tactics grid (see      
       8 +[A Tactic is a Tactic Template plus players](2026-09-29-tactic-
         +templates-and-grid-cell-slots.md)).                            
       9 +The cells need names, and the game-design tables keyed by the o
         +ld ten Positions (Position Weights,                            
      10 +phase membership) need a key that covers every cell without aut
         +horing 31 tables.                                              
      11 +                                                               
      12 +## Proposal                                                    
      13 +                                                               
      14 +- **Slot type.** A slot is its own type, a (row, column) pair, 
         +not a Position. Rows: GK, SW, D, DM,                           
      15 +  M, AM, F, the same codes as the Line Ratings they are rated a
         +gainst (WB is a line, never a row).                            
      16 +  Columns: L, LC, C, RC, R. Displayed as `D RC`, `AM L`; sorted
         + in pitch order.                                               
      17 +- **Position Weights** are keyed by (row, width), where L and R
         + are wide and LC, C, RC are central:                           
      18 +  twelve tables. GK, D wide, D central, DM central, M wide, M c
         +entral, AM central and F central carry                         
      19 +  over today's GK, DL/DR, DC, DM, ML/MR, MC, AMC and ST weights
         +. SW, DM wide, AM wide and F wide are                          
      20 +  new and authored as design values in the spec.               
      21 +- **Phase** follows the row: GK, SW, D → defence; DM, M → midfi
         +eld; AM, F → attack. Whether a run                             
      22 +  shifts part of a contribution to another phase is the formati
         +ons effort's engine-mapping decision.                          
      23 +                                                               
      24 +## Alternatives considered                                     
      25 +                                                               
      26 +- **Weights per cell (31 tables).** Rejected: LC, C and RC of o
         +ne row ask the same of a player, and                           
      27 +  L and R mirror each other.                                   
      28 +- **Weights per row only.** Rejected: a full-back and a centre-
         +back need different attributes.                                
      29 +- **Keep Position as the slot type.** Rejected by the Tactic do
         +main model: it cannot tell two                                 
      30 +  centre-backs apart or express wide DM, AM and F cells.       
      31 +                                                               
      32 +## Acceptance criteria                                         
      33 +                                                               
      34 +- Every one of the 31 cells resolves to exactly one weights tab
         +le and one phase.                                              
      35 +- No slot is typed as a Position.                              
      36 +                                                               
      37 +## Risks                                                       
      38 +                                                               
      39 +- The four new weight tables are unvalidated design values unti
         +l balance testing.

     -onto-the-three-phase-engine.md (+1 -0)
      2  
      3  Type: grilling
      4  Blocked by: 05, 06, 07

      8  ## Question

⏺ I've resolved positional tickets 05, 07 and 08, each with an Agent Note. I
  also turned that map's remaining fog into three tickets: player generation
  (09), retraining (10) and the squad position column (11). Formations ticket
  08, the engine effects, is claimed.

  What the engine does today, which is why 08 comes first:
  - Each minute, possession goes by the ratio of the two midfields.
  - The chance of an attack comes from attack against defence, times tempo.
  - The outcome uses fixed odds: 12% goal, 18% big chance, 35% on target, the
    rest missed.
  - The shooter is picked uniformly at random from the attack-phase slots, with
    no attributes involved.
  - Cards come only from a pressing multiplier.
  - There are no fouls, offsides, crosses or long shots.

  Most CM instructions have nothing in the engine to act on. So this round
  decides the framework, and the per-setting effect tables follow as their own
  tickets.

  ❓ Q1 - A chance-creation layer: Should each attack become a pipeline?
  1. Possession, decided by midfield.
  2. An attack attempt, from attack against defence, adjusted by Passing,
     Mentality and Counter Attack.
  3. A chance type: through ball, cross, long shot, running with the ball,
     hold-up and lay-off, or counter. The type is weighted by team and player
     instructions and the creator's attributes.
  4. A creator and a finisher, picked by weight from slot, instructions and
     attributes, not at random.
  5. The outcome, from the finisher's attributes against the defenders' and the
     goalkeeper's.

  ➡️ Yes. It stays three-phase and non-spatial, but without it Cross Ball, Long
  Shots, Try Through Balls and similar have nothing honest to change.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - New events: Should we add a Foul event (cards come from fouls, driven
  by Tackling, Closing Down and aggression) and an Offside event (driven by
  Offside Trap and forward runs)? Every shot and goal event would also record
  its chance type, for commentary and stats. Corners and free kicks would wait
  for the set-pieces decision.

  ➡️ Yes.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - The engine boundary: Should a resolution step outside the engine turn
  the Tactic into numbers? That would be a per-slot vector of behaviour weights
  (cross frequency, forward-run weight, tackle hardness, pressing intensity,
  mentality shift, ...) plus team-level modifiers. The engine would consume
  those numbers and never see instruction values. The step reruns on every live
  change.

  ➡️ Yes. This keeps the existing rule that the engine never reads tactics
  directly, so that part of the Role Rating note stays in force.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - The cost of playing out of position: CM 01/02 reduced a player's
  tactical attributes when his rating for the slot was below 20, more for
  players with low Versatility. Should resolution scale the occupant's
  decision-making and positional attributes (positioning, decisions, teamwork,
  composure) by a factor that falls from 1.0 at suitability 20? Those scaled
  attributes would feed every engine read.

  ➡️ Yes, using CM's own mechanism. This game has no Versatility attribute, so
  the scaling is the same for everyone until Q7 settles attributes.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - Runs: Should a slot with a run target count in the target cell's row
  and phase while the team has the ball, and in its base cell's while it
  doesn't? Suitability would be scored against the target cell in possession, as
  CM 01/02 scored arrows against the target circle.

  ➡️ Yes. That's the honest non-spatial meaning of CM's arrow: where the player
  goes when his team attacks.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Tuning and proof:
  - Every effect size would be a named constant in one tuning table.
  - Each setting would get a directional test: over many seeded matches, the
    expected direction holds.
  - Determinism stays tested.
  - A calibration harness would check league averages against real-football
    targets: roughly 2.5–2.8 goals, 3–4 yellow cards and 20–26 fouls a match.

  ➡️ Yes. The numbers are design values; the targets are what they're tuned to.

  ---

  ❓ Q7 - Attributes the instructions need: CM's instructions read attributes
  this game doesn't have: Long Shots, Off The Ball, Marking, Anticipation,
  Creativity, Versatility, Work Rate. Should we (a) map onto the closest
  existing attributes for now (Shooting for long shots, Positioning for marking,
  Decisions for anticipation, Flair for creativity) and chart CM's full
  attribute set as a separate effort, or (b) add those attributes inside this
  effort?

  ➡️ (a). Changing the attribute set reaches generation, development, ratings,
  scouting and every player screen, so it deserves its own map. It goes on this
  map as out of scope, with the closest-attribute mapping written down so it can
  be swapped later.

  ---

  ❓ Q8 - Splitting the rest: Should the per-setting effect tables become three
  tickets, each with a drafted table for you to approve?
  - 15: team instruction effects
  - 16: player instruction effects
  - 17: formation, run and suitability effects, plus the new events' stats and
    commentary

  ➡️ Yes. Tickets 09, 11 and 12 then wait on the ones they need.

❯ approve all

  commits to, so the supersession is stated correctly.

         + attack happens, fixed shares decide

  Tickets 15 and 16 are claimed. Below are drafted effect tables for you to
  approve or amend. Directions are fixed here. Sizes are relative: Small,
  Medium, Large. The actual numbers are tuning constants set against the
  calibration targets.

  Ticket 15: team instructions (each non-default value compared with its
  default)

  Instruction: Mentality
  Value: ultra def → gung ho
  Effect: Five steps. Each step toward attacking raises our attack-attempt rate
  and forward-run weight (M) and lowers our defence strength (M). Ultra
  defensive also counts AM and F slots partly in defence when out of
  possession.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Passing
  Value: short
  Effect: Keeping possession ↑ (M), attempt rate ↓ (S), through-ball and cross
  weight ↓ (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction:
  Value: direct
  Effect: Attempt rate ↑ (S), through-ball and counter weight ↑ (M), keeping
  possession ↓ (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction:
  Value: long
  Effect: Attempt rate ↑ (M), midfield partly bypassed in the attack roll, cross

  and hold-up weight ↑ (M), keeping possession ↓ (L)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Focus Passing
  Value: flanks / left / right
  Effect: Cross weight ↑ (M); creator weight moves to wide slots, or to one side
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction:
  Value: middle
  Effect: Through-ball and hold-up weight ↑ (M); creator weight moves to central

  slots
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Tackling
  Value: easy
  Effect: Fouls and cards ↓ (M), contact injuries ↓ (S), defence ↓ (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction:
  Value: hard
  Effect: Fouls and cards ↑ (L), contact injuries on both sides ↑ (M), defence ↑

  (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Closing Down
  Value: own half only
  Effect: Defence ↑ (S), fatigue ↑ (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction:
  Value: always
  Effect: Opponent keeps possession less (M), fouls ↑ (M), fatigue ↑ (L),
  opponent through balls succeed more (S)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Offside Trap
  Value: on
  Effect: Opponent through balls and forward runs end Offside more often (L),
  scaled by our defenders' positioning, teamwork and pace against the runner. A
   beaten trap becomes a one-on-one big chance (M).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Zonal Marking
  Value: on (off = man)
  Effect: Zonal: chance defence uses the team's positioning and teamwork
  average,
  fouls ↓ (S). Man: each chance is defended by the nearest marker's tackling,
  strength and pace; stronger against weak finishers, weaker against strong
  ones, fouls ↑ (S).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Counter Attack
  Value: on
  Effect: After a turnover, counter chance weight ↑ (L), scaled by our forwards'

  pace against the opponent's. Settled attempt rate ↓ (S). Bonus grows with the
   opponent's mentality.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Men Behind The Ball
  Value: on
  Effect: Out of possession every slot counts partly in defence (L); attempt
  rate
  ↓ (M). Counters stay slow unless Counter Attack is also on.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Closing Down
  Value: default
  Effect: Baseline, between stand off and own half only

  A player's override replaces the team value in that slot's behaviour only.

  Ticket 16: player instructions

  The principle: "normal" means the engine's baseline, not "never", because CM's
  hint text says unticked players do less. "Often" roughly doubles that
  behaviour's weight for the slot.

  Instruction: The five overrides
  Effect on the slot: Same effects as ticket 15, applied to this slot only.
  Stand
  off (player only): pressing ↓, fouls ↓, fatigue ↓. Mentality moves this
  slot's forward-run weight and defensive share.
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): as in ticket 15
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Marking: specific
  Effect on the slot: The named opponent is picked less often as finisher and
  converts less; the marker's own attacking share ↓
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): positioning, tackling, pace
  against
  the target's
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Distribution (GK)
  Effect on the slot: Long kick: attempt rate ↑ (S), keeping possession ↓ (M).
  Ask defenders to collect: the reverse. Default: the engine mix.
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): gkKicking, passing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Cross From
  Effect on the slot: Deep: crosses happen more but land worse. Touchline:
  crosses land better but happen less. Default: a mix.
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): crossing; touchline also dribbling

  and pace
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Cross Aim
  Effect on the slot: Near post favours quick finishers; far post favours
  heading
  and strength; centre is neutral; man aims at our best header. Default:
  centre.
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): heading, strength, pace of the
  target
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Cross Ball: often
  Effect on the slot: Cross weight when this slot creates ↑
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): crossing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Long Shots: often
  Effect on the slot: Long-shot weight when this slot finishes ↑; low base
  conversion; a miss ends the attack
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): shooting (for Long Shots),
  composure
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Forward Runs: often
  Effect on the slot: Chance to be the finisher ↑; offside risk ↑; defensive
  share after losing the ball ↓; fatigue ↑
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): positioning (for Off The Ball),
  pace, stamina
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Run With Ball: often
  Effect on the slot: Dribble weight ↑; fouls drawn ↑; a failure is a turnover
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): dribbling, pace, acceleration,
  agility, flair
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Try Through Balls: often
  Effect on the slot: Through-ball weight when this slot creates ↑; turnovers ↑;

  receiver offside risk ↑
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): passing, decisions, flair (for
  Creativity)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Free Role: often
  Effect on the slot: Counts partly in the neighbouring rows while in
  possession;
  creator weight ↑; defensive share ↓. With a low Free Role Rating the team
  loses shape and midfield drops slightly.
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): Free Role Rating, flair, decisions
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Instruction: Hold Up Ball: often
  Effect on the slot: Hold-up weight when this slot is the pivot ↑; the next
  finisher comes from midfield more often; attempt rate ↓ (S)
  Attributes that scale it (closest mapping): strength, firstTouch, composure

⏺ Tickets 15 and 16 are resolved and 09, 11 and 17 are claimed. Here's round 6.

  Ticket 09: live changes

  ❓ Q1 - When a change takes effect: No source says whether CM applied a change
  at once or at a stoppage. Its debug strings suggest it re-simulated from the
  moment of the change. Our engine already applies a live command at the start
  of the first minute the manager hasn't seen (M+1), and at the break for
  half-time changes. Should the complete Tactic use that same boundary, with no
  separate "stoppage" concept?

  ➡️ Yes. It matches CM's likely behaviour and the existing rule that play
  already shown can't change.

  ---

  ❓ Q2 - Validation: A change applies before anything in minute M+1 happens, so
  the pitch at that moment is exactly what the manager has seen. Should a live
  ChangeTactics be validated once, on submit, against that state? A player sent
  off can't be assigned, and ten men means one slot stays empty. Substitutions
  made at the same boundary would apply first, then the new slot layout.

  ➡️ Yes. There's no gap between submit and apply for anything to change in.

  Ticket 11: AI tactics

  ❓ Q3 - AI tactical identity: CM staff carried Preferred Formation, Playing
  Mentality (attacking, adventurous, cautious, sitting back), Pressing Style
  (mixed, stand off, closing down), Playing Style (mixed, passing, direct, long)
  and Marking Style (mixed, zonal, man). AI clubs here have no manager person.
  Should each AI club derive these five preferences from the world seed and its
  stature, the way Bound Staff quality is derived, with no stored rows?

  ➡️ Yes. AI clubs then play differently from each other, and the save doesn't
  grow.

  ---

  ❓ Q4 - Choosing a tactic before the match:
  1. Start from the club's preferred template.
  2. If the squad fits it poorly (average suitability below 15), pick the
     template among its variants and the common presets that gives the best XI
     instead.
  3. Map the style preferences onto team instructions.
  4. Shift Mentality one step by relative strength and venue.

  What it may know about the opponent: its Phase Strengths and its last-used
  formation, both public, and nothing hidden.

  ➡️ Accept.

  ---

  ❓ Q5 - Adjusting during the match: A rule table checked at fixed points
  (every 5 minutes, and after goals and red cards), with no randomness:
  - Losing: after 60' Mentality goes up a step; after 75' a second step and the
    template's attacking variant.
  - Winning by one: after 75' Mentality goes down a step; after 85' Men Behind
    The Ball is switched on (CM's "play out time").
  - A red card: switch to a template that drops a forward.
  - Each change emits CM's commentary events ("change to a more attacking
    style", "change formation", ...).

  ➡️ Accept. The thresholds are tuning constants.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Where AI in-match decisions run: AI-versus-AI matches are simulated in
  one go inside the engine, but the engine can't know tactics vocabulary.
  Should the simulation loop take a tactical controller callback, called at the
  fixed points above, that lives outside the engine? It would return a new
  Tactic, which resolution turns back into numbers. For the human's opponent,
  the same controller's changes are journaled as ChangeTactics commands, like a
  human's.

  ➡️ Yes. The engine stays tactics-blind, and the AI changes through the same
  operations and validation a human uses.

  Ticket 17: formation, run and suitability effects (drafted)

  ❓ Q7 - Crowded and empty rows: Today a phase's strength is the average of its
  slots. That means three strikers are no stronger than one, and a back two
  defends like a back four. Should strength be the average rating × a coverage
  factor for how many players the phase has?
  - Coverage is 1.0 at a normal count: 4 in defence, 4 in midfield, 2 in attack.
  - It rises with diminishing returns above that and falls below it.
  - A separate width coverage (players in wide columns) scales how well a phase
    handles and produces crosses.

  ➡️ Accept. Otherwise 2-3-5 and 5-4-1 play almost identically.

  ---

  ❓ Q8 - Runs and the suitability curve:
  - Runs: a player counts fully in his run target's row while his team has the
    ball and fully in his base row otherwise; a running player tires slightly
    faster.
  - Suitability: the attribute factor is 1.0 at 20, drops gently to 15, then
    steeply below it. That's roughly 0.9 at 15, 0.7 at 10 and 0.5 at 1, and it
    applies only to the decision-making and positional attributes.

  ➡️ Accept. The curve's shape is fixed here; its points are tuning constants.

  ---

  ❓ Q9 - Making effects visible:
  - Match stats: fouls, offsides, possession %, and shots split by chance type
    (cross, long shot, through ball, dribble, counter).
  - Commentary templates: Foul, Offside, beaten trap, each chance type, and the
    AI tactical-change and formation-change events.

  These would show on the match and post-match screens.

  ➡️ Accept.
❯ approve all

  Positional ticket 06: what each old Position consumer becomes

  I drafted this from decisions already made:

  ┌─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐

  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Best XI                     │ fills cells by Position Rating adjusted   │
  │                             │ for suitability                           │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Overall Rating              │ best Position Rating among Natural cells  │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │                             │ holds row, column, run and instructions   │

  ❓ Q1 - Approve the table, plus one open item. Contract offers named a Role
  derived from a Position, and I'd planned to replace it with that Position.
  Positions no longer exist, and CM 03/04 contracts didn't name a playing
  position. Should offers (and the PlayerSigned event) drop the positional
  designation entirely?

  ➡️ Approve, and drop it. That also removes the Role successor question from
  the transfers screens.

  Positional ticket 09: generation

  ❓ Q2 - Archetypes: Should a generated player come from an archetype?
  - The archetypes: GK, centre-back, full-back, wing-back, defensive mid,
    central mid, wide mid, attacking mid, wide forward, striker.
  - Each sets which lines reach natural (18–20) and competent (15–17), and draws
    a side pattern (R, L, RL, C, RC, LC, RLC) with archetype-specific odds.
  - Free Role correlates with AM, F and flair.
  - WB is high for wing-backs and some full-backs.

  ➡️ Accept. The odds are tuning constants, and everything stays deterministic
  under the world seed.

  ---

  ❓ Q3 - Squad demand: Should SQUAD_COMPOSITION be replaced by archetype counts
  of about 25 players: GK 3, CB 4, FB 2+2 (one pair per side), DM 2, CM 3, wide
  mid 2, AM 1, wide forward 2, striker 3? Versatile players would cover the
  3-back and 5-back presets. This renumbers every squad slot, a ruleset change,
  which is fine because old saves are refused anyway.

  ➡️ Accept.

  Positional ticket 10: retraining

  ❓ Q4 - Retraining: CM 03/04 let a manager retrain a player's position or
  side. Should we model that?
  - The manager sets one retraining target per player, a line or a side.
  - It rises gradually through the weekly training tick. The rate grows with
    youth and determination, and is a tuning constant.
  - Nothing decays from disuse.
  - Line ratings don't grow just from playing matches.

  ➡️ Accept. CM had retraining, while growth from playing and decay are
  unverified for CM 03/04, so we leave them out rather than invent them. It
  belongs to training, not the end-of-season Player Development.

  Positional ticket 11: squad screens

  ❓ Q5 - Column, sort and filter:
  - The position column shows the compact label.
  - It sorts by pitch order of the player's best line, then side (R, L, C).
  - A position filter matches "can play": suitability 15 or higher in any cell
    of the chosen rows and sides.
  - Grouping is by the player's best natural line.

  ➡️ Accept.

  ---

  ❓ Q6 - Showing raw ratings: CM showed the label, not the line and side
  ratings, which only the editor exposed. Should raw ratings stay hidden
  everywhere? The Tactics screen would show per-cell suitability as a fit
  indicator where Role Rating used to be.

  ➡️ Yes. It's CM-faithful, and the label plus fit indicator answer the
  manager's real question: "can he play there?"

❯ approve all

       1 +# Agent Note: Line and side ratings change only through retrain
         +ing                                                            
       2 +                                                               
       3 +Status: proposed                                               
       4 +                                                               
       5 +## Problem                                                     
       6 +                                                               
       7 +Players now store CM 03/04's Line and Side Ratings (see

         +e/2026-09-29-players-store-cm-line-and-side-ratings.md)).      
       9 +Something has to decide whether those ratings ever change. CM 0
         +3/04 let a manager retrain a                                   
      10 +player's position or side; whether ratings also grew from playi
         +ng in a position, or decayed from                              
      11 +disuse, is not documented for CM 03/04 (see the                
      12 +[positional research](../../../../docs/research/player-position
         +al-model-cm0304-positional-fields.md)).                        
      13 +                                                               
      14 +## Proposal                                                    
      15 +                                                               
      16 +The manager may set one retraining target per player: a line or
         + a side. That rating rises gradually

         +etermined players; the rate is a                               
      18 +tuning constant. Nothing decays from disuse, and ratings do not
         + grow from match minutes. Retraining                           
      19 +belongs to training, not to the end-of-season Player Developmen
         +t pass.                                                        
      20 +                                                               
      21 +## Alternatives considered                                     
      22 +                                                               
      23 +- **Growth from playing in a position.** Rejected for now: unve

  ❓ Q1 - Set pieces: The tactics-and-match-preparation effort's decision
  request 01 asks whether set pieces ship. CM 03/04 had:
  - Team set-piece instructions per side: corners (short, near post, far post,
    edge of area, edge of six-yard box), free kicks (short, long, cross
    near/far/centre, aim for best header), throw-ins (short, long, quick).
  - Per-player set-piece roles, for attacking and defending.
  - Priorities: penalty, free kick (left/right), corner (left/right) and
    throw-in (left/right) takers.

  Should this effort take on set pieces? That would mean answering that decision
  request with its Option A, and adding a ticket 18 for the set-piece model.
  The engine would gain Corner, Free Kick and Penalty events, arising from
  blocked shots, cleared crosses and fouls.

  ➡️ Yes. A production-level CM clone without set pieces isn't one, and the
  Tactics Overview's "No set pieces configured" panel finally gets its model.
  The team instructions and player roles would sit in Tactic Templates, but the
  takers name players, so they'd sit only on the live Tactic.

  effect on morale would stay out of scope.
